It feels really good when I'm in that zone. But... then someone will slow me down. Like my wife, or one of the folks down at the senior center, if they question how big the display is getting. And then I just snap at them. I get real irritable if they get in the way of what I'm trying to do.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that you've been talking much more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have actually. It happened just the other day at the senior center meetings. I was trying to explain the whole vision for the memorial—you know, the songs I picked out, the quilts we're going to display, all the old photos—and I guess I just got going. Before I even realized it, I had talked right over three different people. My pastor actually had to step in and gently say, 'Aaron, let’s let someone else speak.' So, yes, I suppose I have been talking a lot more than usual when I get on the subject.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more important or especially gifted than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I'm just a regular person. I don't think I have any special gifts or anything like that. I'm just doing what needs to be done to honor my brother, you know? I don't feel more important than anybody else, I just have a job to do with this memorial, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts been racing lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have. My mind just keeps jumping from one idea right to the next. Like, one minute I'm thinking about how to set up a display case for the memorial, and then... well, then I'm suddenly planning a road trip to track down a cousin of ours. And right after that, I'm focused on a verse from an old ballad I want to include. \n\nIt just goes so fast. I try to get it all down on paper so I don't forget, but I honestly can't slow my head down enough to write any of it out in order.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more active than usual in getting things done lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been. I just... I can't seem to sit down in my chair like I used to. I'm constantly up and moving around. I've been hauling boxes, making phone calls, rearranging all these papers for the memorial. I just keep walking from room to room. It honestly feels like my body has more steam in it right now than a man my age ought to have. I just can't stop moving and getting things done.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But my sleep seems about the same as it always is, as far as I know.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high, euphoric, or full of energy lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose I do have a lot of energy right now. Sometimes I just feel... lit up, you know? Full of purpose. Like I finally have this clear vision and the whole town is going to see this memorial and finally understand our family. It feels really good when I'm in that zone. But... then someone will slow me down. Like my wife, or one of the folks down at the senior center, if they question how big the display is getting. And then I just snap at them. I get real irritable if they get in the way of what I'm trying to do.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that you've been talking much more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have actually. It happened just the other day at the senior center meetings. I was trying to explain the whole vision for the memorial—you know, the songs I picked out, the quilts we're going to display, all the old photos—and I guess I just got going. Before I even realized it, I had talked right over three different people. My pastor actually had to step in and gently say, 'Aaron, let’s let someone else speak.' So, yes, I suppose I have been talking a lot more than usual when I get on the subject.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more important or especially gifted than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I'm just a regular person. I don't think I have any special gifts or anything like that. I'm just doing what needs to be done to honor my brother, you know? I don't feel more important than anybody else, I just have a job to do with this memorial, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts been racing lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have. My mind just keeps jumping from one idea right to the next. Like, one minute I'm thinking about how to set up a display case for the memorial, and then... well, then I'm suddenly planning a road trip to track down a cousin of ours. And right after that, I'm focused on a verse from an old ballad I want to include. \n\nIt just goes so fast. I try to get it all down on paper so I don't forget, but I honestly can't slow my head down enough to write any of it out in order.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more active than usual in getting things done lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been. I just... I can't seem to sit down in my chair like I used to. I'm constantly up and moving around. I've been hauling boxes, making phone calls, rearranging all these papers for the memorial. I just keep walking from room to room. It honestly feels like my body has more steam in it right now than a man my age ought to have. I just can't stop moving and getting things done.\n\nDoctor: Did these changes in your energy and behavior start after your brother died?\n\nPatient: Yes, they did. It's been going on for about two months now. Right after he passed away, I started working on his tribute... and well, that's exactly when all this started.\n\nDoctor: Have these changes caused serious problems in your day-to-day life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes, they really have. I've... well, I've completely missed my walking group and the book club I usually go to. I've just let the garden go to weeds, too. And I guess I've upset some friends down at the senior center, because... well, everything I say or do has just become about this memorial display. \n\nAnd my wife... she says our whole day just revolves around her trying to calm me down. She tells me she constantly has to step in and redirect me to stop me from making another phone call, or leaving on another trip, or getting into an argument with somebody. So, yes, it's definitely taking a toll on things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things lately that are unusually risky or impulsive for you, like spending too much money or making reckless decisions?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't done anything like that. I mean... I'm not spending money I don't have or doing anything dangerous. All my focus is just on getting this memorial right for my brother. I haven't been buying things I don't need or making any reckless choices. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard or seen things related to your brother that other people say aren’t there?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, actually. I mean, I still go to bed around my usual time. I haven't really noticed needing any less sleep... I just wake up and get right back to working on the plans for my brother. But my sleep seems about the same as it always is, as far as I know.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high, euphoric, or full of energy lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose I do have a lot of energy right now. Sometimes I just feel... lit up, you know? Full of purpose. Like I finally have this clear vision and the whole town is going to see this memorial and finally understand our family. It feels really good when I'm in that zone. But... then someone will slow me down. Like my wife, or one of the folks down at the senior center, if they question how big the display is getting. And then I just snap at them. I get real irritable if they get in the way of what I'm trying to do.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that you've been talking much more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have actually. It happened just the other day at the senior center meetings. I was trying to explain the whole vision for the memorial—you know, the songs I picked out, the quilts we're going to display, all the old photos—and I guess I just got going. Before I even realized it, I had talked right over three different people. My pastor actually had to step in and gently say, 'Aaron, let’s let someone else speak.' So, yes, I suppose I have been talking a lot more than usual when I get on the subject.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more important or especially gifted than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I'm just a regular person. I don't think I have any special gifts or anything like that. I'm just doing what needs to be done to honor my brother, you know? I don't feel more important than anybody else, I just have a job to do with this memorial, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts been racing lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have. My mind just keeps jumping from one idea right to the next. Like, one minute I'm thinking about how to set up a display case for the memorial, and then... well, then I'm suddenly planning a road trip to track down a cousin of ours. And right after that, I'm focused on a verse from an old ballad I want to include. \n\nIt just goes so fast. I try to get it all down on paper so I don't forget, but I honestly can't slow my head down enough to write any of it out in order.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more active than usual in getting things done lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been. I just... I can't seem to sit down in my chair like I used to. I'm constantly up and moving around. I've been hauling boxes, making phone calls, rearranging all these papers for the memorial. I just keep walking from room to room. It honestly feels like my body has more steam in it right now than a man my age ought to have. I just can't stop moving and getting things done.\n\nDoctor: Did these changes in your energy and behavior start after your brother died?\n\nPatient: Yes, they did. It's been going on for about two months now. Right after he passed away, I started working on his tribute... and well, that's exactly when all this started.\n\nDoctor: Have these changes caused serious problems in your day-to-day life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes, they really have. I've... well, I've completely missed my walking group and the book club I usually go to. I've just let the garden go to weeds, too. And I guess I've upset some friends down at the senior center, because... well, everything I say or do has just become about this memorial display. \n\nAnd my wife... she says our whole day just revolves around her trying to calm me down. She tells me she constantly has to step in and redirect me to stop me from making another phone call, or leaving on another trip, or getting into an argument with somebody. So, yes, it's definitely taking a toll on things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things lately that are unusually risky or impulsive for you, like spending too much money or making reckless decisions?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't done anything like that. I mean... I'm not spending money I don't have or doing anything dangerous. All my focus is just on getting this memorial right for my brother. I haven't been buying things I don't need or making any reckless choices. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard or seen things related to your brother that other people say aren’t there?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, actually. Sometimes when I'm sitting at the kitchen table, you know, sorting through his old song sheets for the display... I'll hear him. \n\nI hear his voice, clear as day, just humming right behind me. Or sometimes he's telling me which tune is supposed to come next. My wife says it's just the grief playing tricks on me, because she doesn't hear a thing when she's in the room. But to me... it sounds real.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your brother is truly speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I do. I mean... I know it sounds odd when I say it out loud. But I’m certain those old ballads my brother kept, they aren’t just songs. They’ve got warnings tucked inside them, specifically for me. My wife keeps saying I’m just reading too much into it, but... well, I still know what I heard in those words. I really believe he's trying to tell me something through them.\n\nDoctor: Before all this extra energy and memorial planning started, had you already been hearing your brother’s voice?\n\nPatient: No, I hadn't. Before I got swept up in all this memorial work after he passed... I never heard his voice in the house. And I never felt like those songs were sending me personal warnings, either. All of that—the humming, the messages in the ballads—it all started at the exact same time as this burst of energy and the nonstop planning I've been doing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I just can't stop moving and getting things done.\n\nDoctor: Did these changes in your energy and behavior start after your brother died?\n\nPatient: Yes, they did. It's been going on for about two months now. Right after he passed away, I started working on his tribute... and well, that's exactly when all this started.\n\nDoctor: Have these changes caused serious problems in your day-to-day life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes, they really have. I've... well, I've completely missed my walking group and the book club I usually go to. I've just let the garden go to weeds, too. And I guess I've upset some friends down at the senior center, because... well, everything I say or do has just become about this memorial display. \n\nAnd my wife... she says our whole day just revolves around her trying to calm me down. She tells me she constantly has to step in and redirect me to stop me from making another phone call, or leaving on another trip, or getting into an argument with somebody. So, yes, it's definitely taking a toll on things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things lately that are unusually risky or impulsive for you, like spending too much money or making reckless decisions?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't done anything like that. I mean... I'm not spending money I don't have or doing anything dangerous. All my focus is just on getting this memorial right for my brother. I haven't been buying things I don't need or making any reckless choices. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard or seen things related to your brother that other people say aren’t there?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, actually. Sometimes when I'm sitting at the kitchen table, you know, sorting through his old song sheets for the display... I'll hear him. \n\nI hear his voice, clear as day, just humming right behind me. Or sometimes he's telling me which tune is supposed to come next. My wife says it's just the grief playing tricks on me, because she doesn't hear a thing when she's in the room. But to me... it sounds real.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your brother is truly speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I do. I mean... I know it sounds odd when I say it out loud. But I’m certain those old ballads my brother kept, they aren’t just songs. They’ve got warnings tucked inside them, specifically for me. My wife keeps saying I’m just reading too much into it, but... well, I still know what I heard in those words. I really believe he's trying to tell me something through them.\n\nDoctor: Before all this extra energy and memorial planning started, had you already been hearing your brother’s voice?\n\nPatient: No, I hadn't. Before I got swept up in all this memorial work after he passed... I never heard his voice in the house. And I never felt like those songs were sending me personal warnings, either. All of that—the humming, the messages in the ballads—it all started at the exact same time as this burst of energy and the nonstop planning I've been doing.\n\nDoctor: Before your brother died, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt unusually energized or driven like this?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. I mean... before he passed, I was just living a pretty quiet life, you know? Taking it easy. I never had a time where I felt so wired or felt like I had to get so much done all at once. This whole thing... this energy I have right now... it's really the first time I've ever felt anything like this.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Or sometimes he's telling me which tune is supposed to come next. My wife says it's just the grief playing tricks on me, because she doesn't hear a thing when she's in the room. My wife keeps saying I’m just reading too much into it, but... well, I still know what I heard in those words. I really believe he's trying to tell me something through them.\n\nDoctor: Before all this extra energy and memorial planning started, had you already been hearing your brother’s voice?\n\nPatient: No, I hadn't. Before I got swept up in all this memorial work after he passed... I never heard his voice in the house. And I never felt like those songs were sending me personal warnings, either. All of that—the humming, the messages in the ballads—it all started at the exact same time as this burst of energy and the nonstop planning I've been doing.\n\nDoctor: Before your brother died, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt unusually energized or driven like this?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. I mean... before he passed, I was just living a pretty quiet life, you know? Taking it easy. I never had a time where I felt so wired or felt like I had to get so much done all at once. This whole thing... this energy I have right now... it's really the first time I've ever felt anything like this.\n\nDoctor: Since this burst of energy began, have there been any times when you were not feeling so energized or driven but were still hearing your brother’s voice?\n\nPatient: No, they've only happened together. Like I said before, I never heard his voice or got those warnings from the songs before all this energy and memorial planning started. It all came on at the exact same time. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose what brings me in is this memorial I'm putting together for my brother. It's, well, it's gotten a lot bigger than I originally meant it to. \n\nI’ve been spending most of my days just chasing down the right songs, calling folks up, planning out these displays and even some trips. It takes a lot of focus. But my wife... she says I’ve completely pushed aside the garden, my walking group, and just about everything else I usually do. She's the one who thought I should come talk to you about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing less sleep than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I still go to bed around my usual time. I haven't really noticed needing any less sleep... I just wake up and get right back to working on the plans for my brother. But my sleep seems about the same as it always is, as far as I know.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high, euphoric, or full of energy lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose I do have a lot of energy right now. Sometimes I just feel... lit up, you know? Full of purpose. Like I finally have this clear vision and the whole town is going to see this memorial and finally understand our family. It feels really good when I'm in that zone. But... then someone will slow me down. Like my wife, or one of the folks down at the senior center, if they question how big the display is getting. And then I just snap at them. I get real irritable if they get in the way of what I'm trying to do.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that you've been talking much more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have actually. It happened just the other day at the senior center meetings. I was trying to explain the whole vision for the memorial—you know, the songs I picked out, the quilts we're going to display, all the old photos—and I guess I just got going. Before I even realized it, I had talked right over three different people. My pastor actually had to step in and gently say, 'Aaron, let’s let someone else speak.' So, yes, I suppose I have been talking a lot more than usual when I get on the subject.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more important or especially gifted than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I'm just a regular person. I don't think I have any special gifts or anything like that. I'm just doing what needs to be done to honor my brother, you know? I don't feel more important than anybody else, I just have a job to do with this memorial, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts been racing lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have. My mind just keeps jumping from one idea right to the next. Like, one minute I'm thinking about how to set up a display case for the memorial, and then... well, then I'm suddenly planning a road trip to track down a cousin of ours. And right after that, I'm focused on a verse from an old ballad I want to include. \n\nIt just goes so fast. I try to get it all down on paper so I don't forget, but I honestly can't slow my head down enough to write any of it out in order.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more active than usual in getting things done lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been. I just... I can't seem to sit down in my chair like I used to. I'm constantly up and moving around. I've been hauling boxes, making phone calls, rearranging all these papers for the memorial. This whole thing... this energy I have right now... it's really the first time I've ever felt anything like this.\n\nDoctor: Since this burst of energy began, have there been any times when you were not feeling so energized or driven but were still hearing your brother’s voice?\n\nPatient: No, they've only happened together. Like I said before, I never heard his voice or got those warnings from the songs before all this energy and memorial planning started. It all came on at the exact same time. There hasn't been a time where I felt normal or tired but still heard him... it's all tied up with this nonstop drive I've got right now.\n\nDoctor: For most of the day lately, have you been feeling unusually cheerful or elevated in your mood?\n\nPatient: Well, it's a mix. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose what brings me in is this memorial I'm putting together for my brother. It's, well, it's gotten a lot bigger than I originally meant it to. \n\nI’ve been spending most of my days just chasing down the right songs, calling folks up, planning out these displays and even some trips. It takes a lot of focus. But my wife... she says I’ve completely pushed aside the garden, my walking group, and just about everything else I usually do. She's the one who thought I should come talk to you about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing less sleep than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I still go to bed around my usual time. I haven't really noticed needing any less sleep... I just wake up and get right back to working on the plans for my brother. But my sleep seems about the same as it always is, as far as I know.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high, euphoric, or full of energy lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose I do have a lot of energy right now. Sometimes I just feel... lit up, you know? Full of purpose. Like I finally have this clear vision and the whole town is going to see this memorial and finally understand our family. It feels really good when I'm in that zone. But... then someone will slow me down. Like my wife, or one of the folks down at the senior center, if they question how big the display is getting. And then I just snap at them. I get real irritable if they get in the way of what I'm trying to do.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that you've been talking much more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have actually. It happened just the other day at the senior center meetings. I was trying to explain the whole vision for the memorial—you know, the songs I picked out, the quilts we're going to display, all the old photos—and I guess I just got going. Before I even realized it, I had talked right over three different people. My pastor actually had to step in and gently say, 'Aaron, let’s let someone else speak.' So, yes, I suppose I have been talking a lot more than usual when I get on the subject.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more important or especially gifted than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I'm just a regular person. I don't think I have any special gifts or anything like that. I'm just doing what needs to be done to honor my brother, you know? I don't feel more important than anybody else, I just have a job to do with this memorial, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts been racing lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have. My mind just keeps jumping from one idea right to the next. Like, one minute I'm thinking about how to set up a display case for the memorial, and then... well, then I'm suddenly planning a road trip to track down a cousin of ours. And right after that, I'm focused on a verse from an old ballad I want to include. \n\nIt just goes so fast. I try to get it all down on paper so I don't forget, but I honestly can't slow my head down enough to write any of it out in order.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more active than usual in getting things done lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been. I just... I can't seem to sit down in my chair like I used to. I'm constantly up and moving around. I've been hauling boxes, making phone calls, rearranging all these papers for the memorial. I just keep walking from room to room. It honestly feels like my body has more steam in it right now than a man my age ought to have. I just can't stop moving and getting things done.\n\nDoctor: Did these changes in your energy and behavior start after your brother died?\n\nPatient: Yes, they did. It's been going on for about two months now. Right after he passed away, I started working on his tribute... and well, that's exactly when all this started.\n\nDoctor: Have these changes caused serious problems in your day-to-day life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes, they really have. I've... well, I've completely missed my walking group and the book club I usually go to. I've just let the garden go to weeds, too. And I guess I've upset some friends down at the senior center, because... well, everything I say or do has just become about this memorial display. \n\nAnd my wife... she says our whole day just revolves around her trying to calm me down. She tells me she constantly has to step in and redirect me to stop me from making another phone call, or leaving on another trip, or getting into an argument with somebody. I mean... I'm not spending money I don't have or doing anything dangerous. All my focus is just on getting this memorial right for my brother. I haven't been buying things I don't need or making any reckless choices. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard or seen things related to your brother that other people say aren’t there?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, actually. Sometimes when I'm sitting at the kitchen table, you know, sorting through his old song sheets for the display... I'll hear him. \n\nI hear his voice, clear as day, just humming right behind me. Or sometimes he's telling me which tune is supposed to come next. My wife says it's just the grief playing tricks on me, because she doesn't hear a thing when she's in the room. But to me... it sounds real.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your brother is truly speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I do. I mean... I know it sounds odd when I say it out loud. Like, one minute I'm thinking about how to set up a display case for the memorial, and then... well, then I'm suddenly planning a road trip to track down a cousin of ours. And right after that, I'm focused on a verse from an old ballad I want to include. \n\nIt just goes so fast. I try to get it all down on paper so I don't forget, but I honestly can't slow my head down enough to write any of it out in order.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more active than usual in getting things done lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been. I just... I can't seem to sit down in my chair like I used to. I'm constantly up and moving around. I've been hauling boxes, making phone calls, rearranging all these papers for the memorial. I just keep walking from room to room. It honestly feels like my body has more steam in it right now than a man my age ought to have. I just can't stop moving and getting things done.\n\nDoctor: Did these changes in your energy and behavior start after your brother died?\n\nPatient: Yes, they did. It's been going on for about two months now. Right after he passed away, I started working on his tribute... and well, that's exactly when all this started.\n\nDoctor: Have these changes caused serious problems in your day-to-day life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes, they really have. I've... well, I've completely missed my walking group and the book club I usually go to. I've just let the garden go to weeds, too. And I guess I've upset some friends down at the senior center, because... well, everything I say or do has just become about this memorial display. \n\nAnd my wife... she says our whole day just revolves around her trying to calm me down. She tells me she constantly has to step in and redirect me to stop me from making another phone call, or leaving on another trip, or getting into an argument with somebody. So, yes, it's definitely taking a toll on things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things lately that are unusually risky or impulsive for you, like spending too much money or making reckless decisions?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't done anything like that. I mean... I'm not spending money I don't have or doing anything dangerous. All my focus is just on getting this memorial right for my brother. I haven't been buying things I don't need or making any reckless choices. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard or seen things related to your brother that other people say aren’t there?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, actually. Sometimes when I'm sitting at the kitchen table, you know, sorting through his old song sheets for the display... I'll hear him. \n\nI hear his voice, clear as day, just humming right behind me. Or sometimes he's telling me which tune is supposed to come next. My wife says it's just the grief playing tricks on me, because she doesn't hear a thing when she's in the room. But to me... it sounds real.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your brother is truly speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I do. I mean... I know it sounds odd when I say it out loud. But I’m certain those old ballads my brother kept, they aren’t just songs. They’ve got warnings tucked inside them, specifically for me. My wife keeps saying I’m just reading too much into it, but... well, I still know what I heard in those words. I really believe he's trying to tell me something through them.\n\nDoctor: Before all this extra energy and memorial planning started, had you already been hearing your brother’s voice?\n\nPatient: No, I hadn't. Before I got swept up in all this memorial work after he passed... I never heard his voice in the house. And I never felt like those songs were sending me personal warnings, either. All of that—the humming, the messages in the ballads—it all started at the exact same time as this burst of energy and the nonstop planning I've been doing.\n\nDoctor: Before your brother died, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt unusually energized or driven like this?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. I mean... before he passed, I was just living a pretty quiet life, you know? Taking it easy. I never had a time where I felt so wired or felt like I had to get so much done all at once. This whole thing... this energy I have right now... it's really the first time I've ever felt anything like this.\n\nDoctor: Since this burst of energy began, have there been any times when you were not feeling so energized or driven but were still hearing your brother’s voice?\n\nPatient: No, they've only happened together. Like I said before, I never heard his voice or got those warnings from the songs before all this energy and memorial planning started. It all came on at the exact same time. There hasn't been a time where I felt normal or tired but still heard him... it's all tied up with this nonstop drive I've got right now.\n\nDoctor: For most of the day lately, have you been feeling unusually cheerful or elevated in your mood?\n\nPatient: Well, it's a mix. I wouldn't just call it cheerful. It's more like... I'll feel completely lit up and full of purpose one minute. I get this feeling like the whole town is finally going to understand our family once this memorial is done. \n\nBut then... well, if my wife or somebody down at the senior center slows me down, or if they question the display I'm working on, I just snap at them. So I do feel great and driven most of the day, but I get pretty short-tempered if people get in my way.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I've had sad days like anyone else, especially when my brother got sick, but I've never been down in the dumps for weeks at a time. I've never had a depression like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have a special role or mission in carrying out this memorial that other people do not have?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I do. I know it sounds odd to some people, but I'm absolutely certain about it. You see, my brother kept these old ballads, and I know they aren't just songs. They've got specific warnings tucked into them, meant just for me to find and understand. My wife tells me I'm reading too much into it, that I'm making things up, but I know what I heard in those words. It's a message for me to carry out this work."
